## Agent message — 2026-10-26T11:58:58.480Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
Finding: **P2 — parent-scope cancellation does not retire the controller.** In [AdminInspectionController.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/AdminInspectionController.kt), public actions check `closed`, but parent-scope cancellation only cancels `scope`; it does not set `closed` or advance `generation`. `isCurrent()` at line 299 therefore still accepts a late noncooperative callback, and later open/retry actions can publish `Loading` even though their launches cannot run. Treat an inactive child scope as retired in action and callback guards.

The existing retirement test calls `close()` before cancelling its fixture scope, so it does not exercise this path. I found no other material issue in the reviewed files.

Review found a cancellation edge case: after the containing scope is cancelled, a late callback could still update inspection state. I’ll add an active-scope check to the action and callback guards, then rerun the same 18 tests and local checks.

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T12:17:58.953Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_ui_navigation_review
Payload:
- **P2 — Stale image metadata can appear current during a failed refresh.** [AdminInspectionScreens.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/AdminInspectionScreens.kt) displays the retained blob ID and upload time, and lines 100–101 expose the profile-photo link whenever an attachment remains. But [ImageSlotState.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/common/images/ImageSlotState.kt) defines that attachment as *last known*; refresh changes `loadState` to `LOADING` and may leave it `FAILED` without clearing the attachment. The profile-photo navigation guard in [AdminInspectionRoutes.kt](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/AdminInspectionRoutes.kt:45) also accepts it without checking `loadState`. After an attachment has been observed, a refresh failure therefore leaves stale metadata and a current-sounding link visible beside the error. Gate those details, link, and route action on `loadState == READY`; URL-display failure can still retain them because attachment metadata remains confirmed.

No other material findings in the bounded review.

## Codex — 2026-10-26T17:43:36.294Z

You’re right—I incorrectly counted teammate helpers against your completion target.

**Excluding those helpers, the planned Halfway feature implementation is in place.** That includes shared controls, onboarding/profile wiring, campsite setup/discovery, lotteries, booking/reservations, Inbox/Android alerts, and read-only admin screens.

What remains is:

- **Configure Maps and verify the actual map on a device.** The adapter, pin picker, saved-location display, and Apply/Cancel behavior are implemented; real map rendering remains unverified.
- **Final candidate preparation:** verify the intended Firebase cloud configuration and package the demonstration APK.
- **After teammates finish:** integrate their helpers and run the complete multi-account acceptance journey. Those helper bodies remain outside your current implementation scope.

Some actions deliberately show pending behavior until their helpers are integrated; the app is not yet a fully usable end-to-end release.

For Maps, do the following:

1. In Google Cloud Console, select your project, enable billing, and enable **Maps SDK for Android**. Create an API key. [Google’s setup instructions](https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/android-sdk/start)

2. Configure the key’s application restriction as **Android apps**, using these values for builds signed on this machine:

   ```text
   Package:
   io.github.CMPUT301F26stderr.campsbridge

   SHA-1:
   7C:CA:CF:30:77:C9:33:59:85:A2:31:78:0D:F3:EC:5A:48:3B:67:74
   ```

   Restrict its API usage to **Maps SDK for Android**. Other machines may have different debug signing fingerprints. [Google’s restriction guidance](https://developers.google.com/maps/api-security-best-practices)

3. Add this line to **`~/.gradle/gradle.properties`**:

   ```properties
   mapsApiKey=YOUR_KEY
   ```

   I checked: no nonempty key is currently configured there or through the Gradle environment property. Keep the key local.

4. Rebuild and reinstall the app. Our Gradle configuration already puts the key into the manifest. Use a device/emulator with Google Play services and internet access. [Device requirements](https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/android-sdk/start)

For the current implementation, only Maps SDK for Android is needed: location selection uses tapping or dragging a pin, so it does not require device-location permission.

Once configured, the remaining Maps check is: tiles load, stored pins display correctly, tapping/dragging updates the working pin, Apply retains it, and Cancel/Back preserves the original location.

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T18:27:15.500Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/b2_rules_backport_audit
Payload:
**B2’s Firebase changes are not yet in `part3/justin`.** The branches share merge base `53eb750`; the relevant B2 commits are `524044c`, `0ac1f82`, and `41da083`. The current branch’s Firebase rules and tooling remain at the pre-B2 versions.

- **`524044c` — rules-budget refactor.** In B2’s `firebase/firestore.rules`, the lottery update checks are centralized in `validUpdate()` and classify changed fields before evaluating costly authorization branches. This addresses Firestore’s 1,000-expression limit: an exhausted rule can deny a request and make a negative test appear to pass. The intended policy stays the same.

- **`0ac1f82` — rule decomposition and ownership documentation.** It extracts named checks for lottery configuration, membership, draws, offers, entry transitions, reservation cancellation, notification event families, delivery creation, and image upload/publication. Storage rules similarly name object creation and cache referenced documents within a check. `firebase/PERMISSIONS.md` adds a useful boundary table mapping Kotlin operation owners to rule checks, and distinguishes session-scoped work from admin maintenance. The rule-case and Kotlin integration test files were not changed by this commit.

- **`41da083` — retained evaluator evidence.** It moves both existing Firebase suites into `scripts/run-firebase-tests.py`, saves their logs, captures Firestore coverage JSON/HTML and the emulator log before shutdown, and fails if any log reports expression-budget exhaustion. `scripts/check-firebase.sh` invokes this runner inside the disposable emulator session. B2’s workflow also uploads `firebase/tooling/build/reports/` with the integration reports.

**Behavior and adaptation notes:** The rule refactor appears intended to preserve permissions; I found no deliberate permission expansion or restriction in these changes. B2 explicitly preserves empty-diff lottery updates because the previous `hasOnly(...)` checks accepted them (`firebase/firestore.rules:269–273` in B2). Tightening that behavior would be a separate policy change with its own tests.

The current branch adds read-only admin notification-audience inspection. Its repository reads existing events, delivery jobs, accounts, and inbox receipts; B2’s admin read rules already cover those paths. The current audience integration tests should still be included when validating the backport. Current CI has also gained Maps verification, so merge the Firebase-report upload path into its workflow rather than replacing the workflow with B2’s version.

Useful locations:

- Current `part3/justin`: [firestore.rules](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/firebase/firestore.rules), [storage.rules](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/firebase/storage.rules), [check-firebase.sh](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/scripts/check-firebase.sh); `scripts/run-firebase-tests.py` is absent.
- B2 refactored rules: lottery operations at `firebase/firestore.rules:219–357`, event/delivery and image rules at `420–513`; storage helpers at `firebase/storage.rules:5–37`.
- B2 ownership and verification guidance: `firebase/PERMISSIONS.md:33–91`; runner at `scripts/run-firebase-tests.py:11–68`; current CI artifact block at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:129–138` lacks B2’s `firebase/tooling/build/reports/` path.
- Current audience reader: [NotificationAudienceQueries.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/data/notification/NotificationAudienceQueries.kt).

## Codex — 2026-10-26T18:28:36.623Z

The Maps key is now configured locally in `~/.gradle/gradle.properties`.

I reviewed B0, B1, and B2 against `part3/justin`. **Most feature implementation is already here; B2’s subsequent cleanup and verification improvements are the important backports.**

| Batch | What it delivered | Current branch |
|---|---|---|
| B0 | Shared foundation, onboarding, offline debug, repository improvements, stable Android test runner | Already included; runner matches B2 |
| B1 | Profile editors, policies, and debug examples for teammate exercises | Production features included; example files partly present as uncommitted changes |
| B2 | Campsite setup/publication, availability, Maps, owner activation, plus repository and Firebase cleanup | Features included; subsequent cleanup and verification changes still need backporting |

The changes worth bringing over are:

**1. Give persistence operations explicit owners**

From `3ac0207`, simplify the large repository implementations into small public entry points backed by cohesive internal classes:

```text
data/lottery/
  FirestoreLotteryRepository  Public repository implementation
  LotteryQueries             Reads and observations
  LotteryWrites              Drafts, configuration, offers, publication
  LotteryMembership          Join, withdraw, decline
  LotteryDraw                Resumable draw processing
  LotteryCancellation        Cancellation coordinated with drawing

data/moderation/
  FirestoreModerationRepository  Authorize and mark removal
  RemovalWrites                 Transactional marking and job creation
  RemovalProcessor              Resume durable cleanup jobs
```

This separates authorization, normal operations, and recovery processing. Cleanup continues calling the existing booking, lottery, and image owners rather than duplicating their behavior.

The selected refactor requires no persisted schema change. We must retain this branch’s later APIs, particularly the server-confirmed `getOffers()` reader, which B2 does not contain.

**2. Untangle Firebase rules without changing the intended permissions**

From `524044c` and `0ac1f82`:

- Replace long combined conditions with named operation checks for configuration, membership, draws, cancellation, booking, notifications, and image publication.
- Compute changed fields once and reject unrelated operation branches before expensive checks.
- Reuse document data within checks instead of repeatedly expressing the same lookups.
- Separate ordinary participation from authority to finish recorded removal work.
- Apply the same organization to Storage upload rules.

This addresses an actual evaluator-budget problem. The objective is clearer, cheaper evaluation while preserving the permission contract.

Keep one deployable rules file per service. B2 does not introduce a rules generator or fragmented deployment system.

**3. Make Firebase verification expose misleading passes**

From `41da083`, together with the reporting changes in `0ac1f82`:

- Run both existing permission and Kotlin integration suites through `scripts/run-firebase-tests.py`.
- Retain suite logs, emulator logs, and evaluator coverage JSON/HTML before emulator shutdown.
- Fail verification when logs report expression-budget exhaustion—even if a negative permission test otherwise passed.
- Upload those reports through CI.
- Update `PERMISSIONS.md` to map each operation to its Kotlin owner and corresponding rules.

The current branch’s newer notification-audience integration tests must remain included.

**4. Bring over configured Maps verification**

B2 adds actual SDK testing for map loading, tapping, dragging, Apply, and Cancel. Its follow-up fixes wait for native mounting and asynchronous selection callbacks.

Its CI arrangement is useful:

- Ordinary required UI checks explicitly use an empty Maps key.
- An optional manually requested Maps job uses `MAPS_API_KEY` and exercises the real SDK.

That verification harness should come over with its synchronization fixes.

**5. Reconcile the debug exercise examples**

Preserve B1/B2’s approach: reuse production screens/controllers with local records and explicit helper injection. Extend the existing local examples with B2’s availability and campsite-ordering examples, while keeping production helper bindings pending.

Some example files already exist locally, so this needs careful reconciliation.

**Recommended order:** Firebase rules and verification together, repository ownership refactor, then Maps verification and debug examples.

I have configured the local key but have not applied these code backports or run tests during this review. Everything inspected was read-only; existing handoffs remain untouched.

Files: [PERMISSIONS.md](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/firebase/PERMISSIONS.md) · [run-firebase-tests.py](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/scripts/run-firebase-tests.py)

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T19:06:08.847Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_ui_navigation_review
Payload:
1. **Entrant Explore → campsite sections:** Start with **Explore**, click the campsite row by its displayed name, then tap **About**, **Map & location**, **Availability**, or **Campsite rules**. From rules, **Reservation & refund policy** opens the policy child; **Available lotteries** opens the choices child. Existing sequence: `CampsiteDetailFlowTest.sectionsAndPoliciesShareOneSavedReader`; choices and return: `CampsiteLotteryEntryFlowTest.multipleChoicesOpenTheirActualLotteryAndReturnToTheSameParent`. The Explore toolbar is **Search campsites**.

2. **Lotteries → winner and saved reservation:** Tap **Lotteries**, then **Action needed** for a winning lottery without a confirmed used reservation; open its title to see the winner detail. For a consumed win, tap **History** → lottery title → **Reservation <id>** → **View reservation** → **Payment & refund** → Back → **Accepted rules & policy**. Existing coverage: `LotteryEntrantFlowTest.savedReservationAndHistoryCallerSurviveRestoration`. **Waiting** is a separate category. From Profile, tap **Inbox**; stable event tags are `inbox-event-<notificationId>` (see `InboxNavigationTest`).

3. **Owner management → availability, preview, participants:** Tap **Campsites** → **Published** or **Drafts** → campsite name to reach **Manage campsite**. There, **Availability** opens the calendar; **Preview listing** opens the read-only preview. For participants: **Campsite lotteries** → published lottery title → **Participants**. Relevant tests: `CampsiteDetailFlowTest.ownerPreviewNeverExposesEntrantOrEditorActions`, `LotteryListFlowTest.managementFilterReadsActualAllocationsAndCanClear`, and `ParticipantListTest`.

4. **Admin Browse and notifications:** **Browse** → **Profiles** → **Campsite owner** → search field tag `admin-search` → profile row tag `admin-profile:<id>` → **Inspect profile photo**; or **Images** → row tag `admin-image:ACCOUNT:<id>` → **Image inspection**. For notifications, tap **Logs** → search tag `admin-log-search` → event tag `admin-log-event:<id>` → **View recipients**. Recipient rows use `admin-audience-recipient:<accountId>`. Existing coverage is in `AdminInspectionFlowTest`, `NotificationLogTest`, and `NotificationAudienceFlowTest`.

Files: [AdminInspectionFlowTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/AdminInspectionFlowTest.kt) · [NotificationAudienceFlowTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/NotificationAudienceFlowTest.kt) · [NotificationLogTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/administration/NotificationLogTest.kt) · [InboxNavigationTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/inbox/InboxNavigationTest.kt) · [ParticipantListTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/ParticipantListTest.kt)

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T20:18:03.814Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/library_license_sources
Payload:
Confirmed the official OSS-notices plugin is a good candidate: the current Google guide recommends plugin `0.13.0` and UI library `17.5.2`. Its release testing covers AGP 9.2.1 and 9.4.0-alpha01, giving strong—but not exact-patch—evidence for this repo’s AGP 9.4.1. Kotlin is not the plugin’s compatibility surface.

Keep these license distinctions in the credits design:

- Firestore and Storage declare Apache 2.0; Firebase Auth declares the Android SDK License.
- Maps Compose and Maps KTX declare Apache 2.0. The Maps SDK declares the Android SDK License and is also governed by Google Maps Platform terms. Its map view already supplies attribution when visible; don’t obscure it or duplicate it on the credits page.
- Most AndroidX/Compose, Kotlin/coroutines, Coil/OkHttp/Okio, and gRPC families declare Apache 2.0.
- Exceptions include `desugar_jdk_libs` (GPLv2 with Classpath Exception) and a DataStore transitive protobuf artifact (BSD-3-Clause), plus Google Play APIs with their own terms.

The OSS plugin gathers POM and embedded Google/Firebase notices, but it skips artifacts without POM license entries. Review its generated output for completeness. It does not cover Unsplash photos; keep per-photo source/photographer credits separately.

Sources: [Google OSS notices guide](https://developers.google.com/android/guides/opensource), [plugin AGP test matrix](https://github.com/google/play-services-plugins/pull/437), [Maps SDK attribution policies](https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/android-sdk/policies), and [Unsplash License](https://unsplash.com/license).

Clear post-fix regression candidates include the three tests added in `35750e2`—listed at the old source lines `LotteryMembershipActionTest:514`, `LotteryMembershipControllerTest:225`, `LotteryMembershipFlowTest:163`—and `LotteryDrawActionTest:320`, identified as a later cancellation regression in `doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:239`. The two booking cancellation tests at `BookingViewModelTest:750,769` and the review-added initial-month test at `:559` are also identified in `doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:97`.

   Several other deletions appear to remove planned behavior: the two superseded Join/Leave tests (`LotteryMembershipActionTest:177,202`) are covered by the frozen participation contract at `doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:235`; changed-quote and stale-callback tests map to `doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:1133-1137`; cross-month booking is specified at `doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:335`. Keep those contracts, perhaps with fewer duplicate cases.

3. **Unresolved:** The plan documents three review-added known-consumption regressions but does not identify their exact methods. The two currently deleted methods in `LotteryDetailEntrantTest:224,239` are plausible matches. Also, three task-example UI tests were added in `e478dcb` but are not named by the frozen B2 test list at `doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:1863`; whether to retain them depends on treating task-catalogue UI coverage as feature verification or out-of-scope testing.

Preserve representative tests for the frozen booking and lottery contracts, then resolve the known-consumption and task-example ambiguities before deleting more.

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T20:38:54.798Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/review_revision_cleanup
Payload:
**P2 — Firestore rules let an owner reset the lottery revision and bypass the publication race guard.** In [firestore.rules](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/firebase/firestore.rules), `ownerConfigurationUpdate` authorizes `_revision` changes without checking their value. The owner-update branch at line 284 permits `_revision` alongside editable fields, while `matchingRevision` relies on the counter increasing by exactly one.

Reproduction: `publish()` reads revision 0 and offer set A. A concurrent `setOffer()` replaces an offer and advances the revision to 1. An owner write changing the title and resetting `_revision` to 0 passes the rules; the publication transaction then sees revision 0 and can publish using its stale offer set. That defeats the guard’s purpose. The Kotlin writer increments correctly, but rules should enforce revision semantics for direct owner writes too.

The strict revision handling otherwise looks consistent: new drafts write revision 0; edit and offer writes require the stored integer and advance it; lottery creation and updates are rejected when the revision is absent or invalid. The schema and tooling docs now describe reseeding without old-schema compatibility. The specified test removals are scoped to the named cases and do not warrant restoring them or adding tests.

Compose libraries without individual versions inherit the Compose BOM.

**Compatibility points**

- The app has `minSdk = 24` and `targetSdk = 37` in [app/build.gradle.kts](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/build.gradle.kts). Navigation 2.10 requires API 24, so the current minimum meets that floor.
- Maps Compose 9.0.1’s published Gradle module metadata requests Kotlin stdlib 2.4.20 and Compose BOM 2026.09.00. This makes the Kotlin/compiler upgrade a prerequisite to moving from Maps Compose 6.12.0; the Maps Compose README lists Android API 21+.
- Firebase BOM 35.1.0 manages Firebase Auth 25.0.0. The catalog’s strict 24.0.1 pin prevents adopting that BOM-managed version. After the Kotlin upgrade, remove the strict pin and let the BOM select Auth 25.0.0.
- Maps Compose documents that it brings in the matching Maps SDK version. The project also directly declares the Maps SDK; whether that explicit dependency is redundant depends on whether app code directly consumes its APIs.

**Sources:** official [AndroidX stable release tables](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/versions/stable-channel), [Compose BOM mapping](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/bom), [Firebase Android setup and current BOM](https://firebase.google.com/docs/android/setup), [Maps Compose README and releases](https://github.com/googlemaps/android-maps-compose), and official [Google Maven](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/) / [Maven Central](https://repo.maven.apache.org/maven2/) metadata. Representative release pages: [AndroidX Test](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/test), [Lifecycle](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/lifecycle), [DataStore](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/datastore), [Coil releases](https://github.com/coil-kt/coil/releases).

The relevant current pins are in [libs.versions.toml](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/gradle/libs.versions.toml); Google Services is pinned in [build.gradle.kts](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/build.gradle.kts:7), and Foojay in [settings.gradle.kts](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/settings.gradle.kts). The parent audit can combine these results with its separate SDK, AGP, Gradle and Kotlin findings.

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T22:31:56.198Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/firebase_upgrade_isolation
Payload:
1. **Commands:** Read `FirebaseEmulatorRule.kt`, inspected the Firebase integration test XML and captured log, and decompiled cached Firebase Auth 24.0.1 and 25.0.0 bytecode. I did not run Gradle or the emulator suite and made no source edits.

2. **Result:** The captured run has 109 tests: 107 fail, all with `IllegalStateException: FirebaseApp was deleted`. Failures occur at `FirebaseEmulatorRule.actor` line 134, in `auth.useEmulator`. The 2 passing cases are `AccountRepositoryTest.creationIsIdempotentAndOwnerCapabilityIsAdditive` and `OnboardingIntegrationTest.ownerSetupConfirmsCapabilityBeforeEnteringWorkspace`.

3. **Diagnosis:** Each actor gets a unique Firebase app name but reuses the API key `fake-api-key`; teardown deletes each app after its test. Auth 25’s `useEmulator` path maintains a static emulator override cache keyed by API key and notifies previously registered clients when that key is reused. A prior client retains its deleted app, causing the exception. Auth 24 used a different internal path.

   **Proposed minimal fixture fix:** Generate a unique dummy API key for each Firebase app, keeping the existing emulator setup and teardown. The Auth emulator can use fake API keys; no production compatibility shim or SDK rollback is indicated.

4. **Remaining validation:** This fix is based on the captured failure and decompiled SDK behavior, not a rerun. Apply the fixture-only change, then rerun `scripts/check-firebase.sh` to verify it.

Files: [FirebaseEmulatorRule.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/test/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/testsupport/firebase/FirebaseEmulatorRule.kt) · [check-firebase.sh](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/scripts/check-firebase.sh)

`AdminBrowseViewModel` is the right place to resolve gallery URLs: it already owns `ImageRepository`, while `RemoteImage` accepts a plain reference and handles Coil loading and failure display. Extend the existing image presentation state with each attachment’s transient URL/status, scoped to the Images tab. Keep repositories out of composables; avoid creating one full `ImageSlotController` per tile.

Browse state currently has only attachment target/blob metadata, so parent names are unavailable there. The image detail screen already observes one account or campsite parent, but the gallery’s prototype expects parent names under its two-column thumbnails. Decide whether the gallery should join parent records for captions, and how to label missing parents.

Relevant existing tests are `AdminBrowseViewModelTest.imageSearchNeverLoadsMetadataOrUrls`—which assumes URL resolution does not occur—and the selected-image URL lifecycle tests in `AdminInspectionControllerTest`. The browse test and fixtures would need updating if URL hydration and parent labels become part of the gallery.

Inspected all 15 baseline booking states: `BookingPreview` indices 0–9 and `BookingSubmissionPreview` indices 0–4. I compared the direct references 07.02–07.05, 07.07–07.09 at full size; states without a direct reference were checked against the nearest booking flow. These are **before** screenshots, so they do not verify the later shared typography and app-bar changes.

**Feature-specific differences to address:**

- **Dates, index 0 vs 07.02:** The app shows the deadline and date guidance as plain text and has no compact selected-stay summary; the prototype groups the deadline, calendar, and selected dates/price into Material surfaces. Keep the agreed shared `CampsiteCalendar`; focus any changes on surrounding hierarchy and selection summary. The calendar implementation is in [StayDatesStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/steps/StayDatesStep.kt).

- **Guests, index 1 vs 07.03:** The app shows both a number-entry field and a separate “Guests” stepper, repeating the count across two controls. The prototype presents one counter card with the campsite/date context and capacity. Consolidate the controls into one clear Material component, keeping direct typing only if it can fit naturally into that component. See [GuestsStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/steps/GuestsStep.kt).

- **Rules, index 2 vs 07.04:** The app presents rules and payment information as ungrouped text, followed by a policy link and acceptance checkbox. The reference groups rules and refund information into distinct sections. Apply that grouping to the actual campsite rules and existing policy; don’t copy the prototype’s sample rules into app data. See [TermsStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/steps/TermsStep.kt).

- **Review, index 3 vs 07.05:** The app uses a plain vertical summary, repeats the campsite name as both the step heading and a summary field, and has no campsite image. The prototype uses a campsite feature card, compact rows with edit actions, and a distinct total/payment surface. Group the existing information into that hierarchy and show the campsite name once. See [BookingReviewStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/steps/BookingReviewStep.kt) and [StaySummary.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/summaries/StaySummary.kt).

- **Changed-review state, index 9 vs 07.07:** The preview is described as a changed-quote case, but its fixture still has a CAD 130 quote; it does not establish the reference’s CAD 130-to-CAD 140 change. The screenshot shows the old amount and a rules-change message, so it does not prove the changed-price presentation. Make the preview use a genuinely changed amount, then verify the updated-price prompt and explicit re-acceptance. The fixture is in [BookingPreviews.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingPreviews.kt).

- **Conflict state, submission index 4 vs 07.09:** The preview displays the conflict message on the review screen with a green “Confirm reservation” action. The actual ViewModel moves a conflict back to the dates step, so this is a misleading preview state rather than evidence of the production transition. Make the preview reflect the real dates-step state and compare it to the reference’s date warning and “Choose dates” action. The preview currently combines a review draft with a rejected submission at [BookingPreviews.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingPreviews.kt); the real conflict handling is in [BookingViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingViewModel.kt).

**State/fixture notes:**

- Indices 4–6 (loading, opportunity-read failure, booking closed) have no matching 07.* reference. Their concise loading/error/unavailable presentation is reasonable; there is no direct prototype mismatch to fix.
- Indices 7–8 (eligibility pending and inventory failure) also lack a direct reference. Both screenshots show an enabled green “Next” even though continuation is rejected by ViewModel validation. Consider disabling it until dates are valid, or make the validation response clearly actionable. The UI enables the footer for every non-review step at [BookingScreen.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingScreen.kt), while advancement can reject the action at [BookingViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingViewModel.kt).
- Submission indices 0–3 show progress, recovery, unknown outcome, and confirmed absence. Only index 0 maps directly to 07.08; the other states are useful recovery cases without dedicated references. Keep them clearly distinguished and retain the review summary.
- Several sample values differ from the references: the app preview says “River Camp” and 3 guests, while the prototypes show “Pine Creek” and 4; the booking deadline also differs. Align preview fixtures with reference values where practical so screenshots compare the same scenario.

I did not flag calendar-cell styling or weekday order; those belong to the agreed shared calendar normalization. No code, tests, Gradle, or ADB state was changed.

## Agent message — 2026-10-26T23:42:05.172Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/review_booking_visuals
Payload:
Inspected all 11 baseline states from the provided screenshots. These findings apply to the captured baseline; they do not verify the current rendered screens. I did not run builds or tests.

- **Lottery list, index 0 → prototype 12.01:** The owner preview has Published/Drafts tabs and a single upcoming card with its title and deadline. The reference has Draft/Open/History tabs and lottery cards showing campsite, location, offered dates, deadline, and a Manage action. Reconcile the category labels and the key details needed to distinguish lotteries. The preview shows **3:00 PM UTC**, while setup/review use the campsite’s America/Edmonton zone and the prototype shows MDT; make the list deadline use the same relevant zone. The empty/loading/failure states at indices 1–3 have no direct prototype references.

- **Lottery list, index 1:** The empty-state message begins flush against the left edge, unlike the inset content in the reference. Add a body inset or center the message.

- **Lottery list, index 4:** The intentional missing-reference state displays the raw ID `unavailable-draw` below “Linked lottery is unavailable.” Replace or omit the internal ID in user-facing copy.

- **Lottery setup, index 0 → prototype 11.01:** The basics form covers the same task; no feature-specific visual defect stood out.

- **Lottery setup, index 1 → prototype 11.02:** The reference lets owners select and allocate nights across two campsites. The app shows one selected campsite and one night set (`LotteryDraft.campsiteId`, `LotteryCampsiteStep`). Confirm whether multi-campsite lotteries are in scope; this is a capability mismatch, not just a layout difference.

- **Lottery setup, indices 2–3 → prototypes 11.04–11.05:** The app presents opening and closing date/time fields as separate tall controls. The prototypes group date and time within each opening/closing section and show a timezone/duration summary. Grouping the controls would make each window easier to scan. I did not flag the calendar rendering; the shared calendar is an intentional normalization.

- **Lottery setup, index 4 → prototype 11.06:** The prototype includes a registration-limit switch and capacity field. The app says “Unlimited registrations”; it has no cap control, and setup writes `waitlistCapacity = null` ([LotteryWinnersStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/steps/LotteryWinnersStep.kt), [LotterySetupViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotterySetupViewModel.kt)). The domain model supports a capacity, so this is a feature mismatch to resolve.

- **Lottery setup, index 5 → prototype 11.07:** The prototype presents five grouped summaries with clear edit actions; the app shows mostly ungrouped values, including each night on its own line. Group the review by setup step so it is easier to scan. The pending schedule validation and disabled publish action are deliberate for this preview state.

Files: [LotteryCampsiteStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/steps/LotteryCampsiteStep.kt)

I inspected all 18 `LotteryDetailPreview` indices against the mapped prototype renders. The single-campsite and unlimited-registration choices match the Halfway plan and are not findings. This is a baseline audit; the current rendered screens remain unverified.

The main mismatch is page structure. States **0–14** render status, membership or draw actions, site allocation, and both schedule windows together in one vertical scroll ([LotteryDetailScreen.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryDetailScreen.kt)). References **06.02–06.04** split the overview, schedule, and offered-campsite details into navigable pages; **12.02, 12.08–12.09, and 12.12** show owner management sections. The app currently has no schedule or offered-site detail screen. Add a compact overview with the current status, relevant deadline, and summary, then link to those detail pages. Keep the state-specific primary action visible in a fixed footer, as in the references. The current action buttons sit inside the scrollable content and can scroll away.

State-specific findings:

- **0 (draft, 12.12):** The Continue setup action is present, but the preview shows all configuration inline instead of the owner management sections and review/publish action.
- **1 (upcoming, 06.09/12.02):** Shows both “Published” and “Upcoming.” The reference presents the current phase and next deadline; it also offers browse/join affordances. Use one clear phase status and surface the relevant deadline.
- **2 (draw pending, 06.11):** Shows a generic “Published” status and “Results pending,” but does not clearly label the phase as “Drawing,” as the reference does.
- **3 (booking open, 06.07):** The fixture says the entrant won and has an unused opportunity, but the preview omits “Choose dates.” `onBooking` is null in the preview call, and the screen only shows this action when that callback is supplied ([LotteryDetailScreen.kt:178–190, 499](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryDetailScreen.kt)). Supply a preview callback so this required state’s main action is visible. Decline is correctly out of scope.
- **4–5 (membership/opportunity read failures):** Retry feedback is visible, and withholding join/booking actions until the reads resolve is appropriate.
- **6 (eligible to join), 7 (rejoin in progress), 8 (leave confirmation), 9 (rejoin failure):** The fixtures show the expected membership states and feedback. Their actions remain in the scroll body; the site and dates appear later, unlike the reference’s detail links followed by a fixed action area.
- **10 (published lottery with no offers):** This is a deliberate anomaly fixture, but it appears as a normal published/upcoming lottery while saying no nights were allocated. Present it as an unavailable or inconsistent saved listing, rather than an ordinary upcoming lottery.
- **11 (offer read failure):** The retryable failure is intentional; it should remain distinct from the missing-offer state.
- **12–14 (draw available, drawing, draw failed; 12.08–12.09):** Draw/retry feedback is present, but owner management sections are absent and the action is embedded in the long detail scroll. The in-progress state should communicate “Drawing” distinctly from the pending-results message. The owner preview also omits `onParticipants`, so it cannot show that management destination.
- **15–17 (loading, failure, unavailable):** These are intentional standalone read states; no feature-specific visual issue stood out.

Across the content states, the site allocation and schedule are hard to scan: each night is printed separately, the night count repeats that information, and schedule ranges include seconds. The child pages should group the registration and booking windows by opening/closing time in the campsite’s zone, and show the single offered campsite with its allocated nights compactly. No files were changed; I did not run Gradle or ADB.

### Findings

- `BookingViewModel` has no image dependency or photo state. Its constructor takes the entrant, lottery and campsite IDs; lottery, campsite and booking repositories; a clock; `SavedStateHandle`; and the optional reserved eligibility function. The production factory calls it in [ViewModelFactories.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/ViewModelFactories.kt).
- The booking review screen renders a stay summary, edit links, accepted-terms status, policy link, changed-quote approval and simulated-payment text. It has no photo field or image callback in [BookingReviewStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/steps/BookingReviewStep.kt).
- Booking constructor callsites to account for are: production factory at `ViewModelFactories.kt:81`; debug demo at [TaskExampleSession.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/debug/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/demo/tasks/TaskExampleSession.kt); test host at [TestAppHost.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/testsupport/TestAppHost.kt); unit tests at `BookingConfirmationTest.kt:542`, `BookingDateSelectionTest.kt:207`, `BookingPresentationStateTest.kt:180`, `BookingViewModelTest.kt:743`, and `BookingWizardViewModelTest.kt:494`; and the device test factory at `BookingFlowTest.kt:470`.
- `CampsiteDetailViewModel` is the existing read-only image lifecycle example: after the campsite is confirmed presentable, it starts an `ImageSlotController` for `ImageTargetType.CAMPSITE`; unavailable or failed campsite reads stop it. It exposes `photoState` and `retryPhoto()` in [CampsiteDetailViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteDetailViewModel.kt). The route collects the photo state with lifecycle awareness at [CampsiteDetailRoute.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteDetailRoute.kt).
- `ReservationDetailViewModel` has no `ImageRepository` or `ImageSlotController`; it reads reservation records through `BookingRepository` only, in [ReservationDetailViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/reservations/ReservationDetailViewModel.kt).
- `ImageSlotController` automatically observes the attachment and resolves a transient display URL. Its public state includes `attachment`, `displayUrl`, URL-loading/failure flags, and its API includes `retryDisplay()`, `retry()` and `close()` in [ImageSlotController.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/common/images/ImageSlotController.kt). `FakeImageRepository` exposes controlled attachment flows, URL results/failures/gates, and records observed targets and downloaded blob IDs in [FakeImageRepository.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/debug/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/testsupport/fakes/FakeImageRepository.kt).
- I found no booking or reservation test assertions mentioning photos or images.

### Unresolved questions

- Should the booking review show the current campsite image, or a historical image tied to the saved reservation? The inspected reservation detail currently presents the saved campsite name and location, while the booking model carries the live campsite.

### Recommended next steps

- Decide which campsite identity/image the review should display, then trace how the booking screen obtains it and how its loading, empty and failed image states should appear.
- Preserve the reserved `availableNights` parameter and update each constructor callsite if the `BookingViewModel` contract changes.
- No code changes or tests were made for this search.

## Codex — 2026-10-27T11:54:03.324Z

**Admin functionality is partly implemented, but workspace switching is already implemented.** It may be hidden in the scenario you’re using.

- **Default Camper demo:** the account only has the Camper role, so no switcher appears.
- **Owner demo:** the account has both Camper and Campsite owner. You can switch using the app-bar dropdown or the Profile “Switch workspace” row.
- **Administrator demo:** open **Demo tools → Administrator**. This loads a separate sample admin account; ordinary accounts don’t acquire admin through the workspace menu.

The implemented admin area includes campsite/profile/image browsing and inspection, image thumbnails, notification logs, event details, and recipient status. It is currently **read-only**. Moderation actions remain for later, and some search/filter helpers remain reserved for teammates.

The HTML comparison page shows captured content states; it doesn’t demonstrate whether a screen is reachable from your current account.

One test gap found:

- **P2 — H06 task wiring has no focused contract test.** [TaskExampleSession.kt:75](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/debug/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/demo/tasks/TaskExampleSession.kt) routes `GUEST_MATCH` to `bindings.matchesGuests`, but [DiscoveryTaskExamplesTest.kt:20](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/f7c29da52e63e201100dae3d94d9f7e300365c5a/app/src/test/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/demo/tasks/DiscoveryTaskExamplesTest.kt) only exercises `TEXT_MATCH`. Even after B5, its production factory leaves date eligibility pending because the reserved H11 helper is not wired. The atomic reservation operation itself is already present on main.

The B6 range has a Git-boundary wrinkle: `818606f..c0c728d` excludes `818606f`, which contains the quote and stay-selection rules. The complete B6 sequence is:

- `818606fcbff402debaa29ce9ecea1161350514c8` — quote and stay-selection rules.
- `d0615bc9e95c4c11b700518f0f8c312e7273c6c7` — scoped calendar reads.
- `901092e8d98886cb432d99319730145ab9bd6ea4` — opportunity validation and draft retention.
- `fec13961a9b3ec847b7d7b75aeaf2c5178426167` — step validation and explicit approvals.
- `2c09d4ba3e93becaafa4410e1a84fffd3ae5994b` — eligibility wiring to calendar actions.
- `b08cb88b69a0dfd42fb083534dfb8da9affb93fc` — action guards and navigation handoffs.
- `05d87e61ef1e95f2594a5d563a7b37516e7ac2d3` — four-step screens and winner navigation.
- `ae1c081e933cc4fb4d8f38e86f803956fc2e1413` — reservation acknowledgement and uncertain-outcome resolution.
- `aa6dfec5968a22b8fb09f55a1c1887f7ae81a92e` — guarded confirmation and restored uncertainty.
- `c0c728d370a4ec8257f9035ce0dd52278f204f09` — confirmation and recovery controls.

The booking path is winner action → booking graph → four-step route → guarded `BookingViewModel.confirm()` → `BookingWriter.submit()` → existing repository transaction. See [LotteryDetailScreen.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryDetailScreen.kt), [BookingNavigation.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/navigation/BookingNavigation.kt), [BookingViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingViewModel.kt), and [BookingWriter.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingWriter.kt). Main already exposes `getForOpportunity` and `reserve` in [BookingRepository.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/domain/booking/BookingRepository.kt); [BookingWrites.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/data/booking/BookingWrites.kt) atomically validates the offer, terms, quote and inventory while creating the reservation, payment and notification. The existing emulator test covers those persisted facts in [BookingRepositoryTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/test/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/integration/firebase/booking/BookingRepositoryTest.kt).

## Codex — 2026-10-27T22:16:07.903Z

**Both are ready to prepare as the next baselines, but neither is ready for an immediate merge.** Their implementations exist on `part3/justin`; we still need curated publication branches and verification. **Merge B5 first, then B6.**

| Baseline | Original bulk to retain | Later changes to bring forward |
|---|---|---|
| **B5: Inbox and saved reservation readers** | `362821e`, `b8d7ca3`, `0c93817`, `bd01807` | Inbox styling from `832b972`; read-only reservation/payment/terms styling from `4701371`; relevant shared stay-summary styling. |
| **B6: Booking and confirmation recovery** | `bd01807..c0c728d`: ten commits, starting with `818606f` | Booking controls/layout from `cf611c2`; review photos and associated wiring from `1737327`; approved booking-test removals from `6e2a2f4`. |

Several of those later commits mix features, so we should backport selected files or hunks.

I would prepare them this way:

1. **Create separate `part3/b5` and `part3/b6` worktrees.** Preserve the original bulk commits and merge accepted `main` into each publication branch. Prepare B6 against main after B5 is accepted.

2. **Resolve shared integration carefully.** The B5 dry merge reports five conflicts around ownership documentation, factories, navigation, and lottery detail. Preserve current styling and completed **H01/H04/H07** bindings. Copying development factories wholesale would replace those bindings with `null`.

3. **Separate the Inbox title dependency cleanly.** The updated Inbox uses title formatting introduced with B8. Extract the shared title mapping without importing Android alert delivery, permissions, or notification-tap handling.

4. **Retain existing persistence and tooling.** Main already contains the repository ownership refactor and current Firebase rules. B5/B6 use those contracts. Preserve main’s newer runner isolation and formatter fixes. Keep B7 cancellation, B8 alerts, B9 administration, and frozen prototype files outside these PRs.

5. **Prepare the corresponding debug examples.** B5 gets H09/H10 examples; B6 gets H11. Their helper implementations remain reserved. Publish their separate handoffs and mark them Ready after the respective baseline is accepted.

6. **Verify each publication tree.** Freeze the checklist first, retain the approved test cleanup, then run build/lint/unit/coverage, relevant Firebase and Compose interactions, and visual checks. Require every mandatory CI check on the exact PR head, then verify APK/site publication after merging.

After both merges, merge accepted `main` back into `part3/justin` to synchronize teammate implementations and tooling.

This was a source/history review; candidate builds and tests have not been rerun.
